 CATEGORICAL USE OF FORCE INVESTIGATIONS AUDIT 
 
In accordance with the Fiscal Year 2008/09 Annual A udit Plan, Internal Audits and Inspections 
Division (IAID) conducted a Categorical Use of Forc e Investigations Audit to evaluate 
categorical use of force investigations for legalit y and conformance with the Los Angeles Police 
Department procedures.  
 
IAID used a 95 percent compliance threshold to dete rmine Consent Decree compliance for 22 
objectives.  All 22 compliance determinations met o r exceeded the 95 percentage standard.  
Improvement in the compliance rate regarding On-Sce ne Supervision was noteworthy: from 87% 
in the prior audit to 100% in this audit.  Increase s in compliance determinations were mainly the 
result of strategies implemented by Force Investiga tion Division.

iii 
 CATEGORICAL USE OF FORCE SYSTEMS AUDIT 
 
In accordance with the Fiscal Year (FY) 2008/09 Ann ual Audit Plan, Internal Audits and 
Inspections Division (IAID) conducted a Categorical  Use of Force Systems Audit to evaluate 
categorical use of force systems for conformance wi th the Los Angeles Police Department 
(Department) procedures.  
 
Summary of Findings 
 
IAID used a 95 percent compliance threshold to dete rmine Consent Decree compliance for 15 
objectives, and used qualitative compliance thresho lds for four objectives.  Of the 19 objectives, 
13 met compliance standards, and 6 did not.   
 
The most notable decrease from the prior year audit  was regarding submission of Categorical 
Use of Force (CUOF) Reports to the Police Commissio n 60 days prior to statute.  The 
compliance rate decreased from 97% in FY 2007/08 to  48% in the FY 2008/09.  This is a low 
risk issue due to the substantial management contro ls and oversight for the process.  The lack of 
timely notifications regarding one CUOF incident im pacted two objectives, bringing them from 
compliant in the prior year’s audit to non-complian t this year.  One recommendation was made 
as a result of the audit.

v 
 COMPLAINT, FORM 1.28, INVESTIGATIONS AUDIT – PHASE I 
 
In the prior audit, the Los Angeles Police Departme nt  (Department) did not meet the standards 
in 5 of 13 objectives.  In this year’s audit, the D epartment did not meet the standards in 1 of 14 
objectives as indicated below.  The Department came  into compliance regarding the timeliness of 
Internal Affairs Group’s (IAG) review of the compla int, the timeliness of the completion of 
investigations, the staffing of IAG investigator po sitions, and the notification of complaint intake 
to the Office of Inspector General within one week.  
 
Objective No. 2(c) – Continuation of 24-Hour Toll F ree Telephone Complaint Hotline  
Auditors called the hotline 30 times within a 46-da y period.  On 12 occasions, either no one 
answered the telephone or the voice print system di d not pick up the call.     
 
Subsequent to auditing the hotline, IAG indicated t he hotline was serviced and working properly.  
As a result, Civil Rights Integrity Division (CRID)  conducted additional testing to determine 
whether the Voice Print system answered the calls.  Each of the 14 calls made by CRID 
personnel was answered by the voice print system.  Pursuant to an agreement with the 
Independent Monitor, CRID will conduct additional t esting to determine whether the hotline is 
working properly.  (Note: additional testing was co mpleted and the hotline was working 
properly.)  The table on the following page indicat es a summary of findings.

vii 
 COMPLAINT, FORM 1.28, INVESTIGATIONS AUDIT – PHASE II 
 
The Los Angeles Police Department (Department) met the 95 percent compliance threshold for 
26 of the 32 objectives tested.   Results demonstra te improvement from the prior year audit in 
which the Department did not meet compliance standa rds for eight objectives.  Findings 
regarding the remaining six objectives are summariz ed below:      
 
• The compliance rate for Objective No. 5(b) – Interviewing of Witnesses and 
Complainants  in Professional Standards Bureau (PSB) investigatio ns  was 88 % as 28 of 
32 PSB documented interviews of all identified witn esses and complainants.     
 
• The compliance rate for Objective No. 5(f) – Interviewing of Supervisors in PSB 
investigations was 71 % and was based upon a total of seven invest igations.     
   
• The compliance rate for Objective No. 6(d) – Canvassing the Scene to Locate Witnesses 
in Chain of Command (COC) investigations was 88 % as 21 of 24 COC investigations 
documented canvassing of the scene to locate witnes ses.     
 
• The compliance rate for Objective No. 14(a) – Completeness of the Investigation File  was 
93 % as the Department produced all required audio and video recordings for 69 of 74 
investigations.    
 
• The compliance rate for Objective No. 14(c) – Completeness of all Audio/Video Files  was 
94 % as the tape recordings were complete in 66 of 70 investigations. 
 
• The compliance rate for Objective No. 15 – Comparison of Officer and Witness 
Statements  was 88 % as investigator summaries and taped witne ss statements were 
consistent in 61 of 69 investigations.      
  
Conclusion  
 
The Department has generally improved performance f rom the prior year, and compliance rates 
for five of six non-compliant objectives ranged fro m 88 % to 94 %.  However, documentation of 
witness interviews and investigative steps, the abi lity to produce complete audio tapes, and 
accurate summaries of witness statements are import ant to ensuring that commanding officers 
who make disciplinary decisions are provided with c omplete and accurate information.      
 
The table on the following page indicates a summary  of findings.

 GANG ENFORCEMENT DETAIL SELECTION CRITERIA AUDIT 
 
The Los Angeles Police Department met the 95 percen t compliance standard in seven of twelve 
objectives.  Consistent with prior audits, complian ce was withheld in one objective.  For the 
remaining four objectives, compliance rates were be tween 91 % and 94 % and were determined 
to be in the low to moderate risk categories.  Ther e were no high risk findings. The table below 
indicates a summary of findings.

x 
 NON-CATEGORICAL USE OF FORCE INVESTIGATIONS AUDIT  
 
Of the 28 objectives measured, 20 met or exceeded t he 95 percent compliance standard.  Of the 
remaining eight objectives, three significantly dec reased from the prior audit; 4 one objective 
significantly increased but remained below the 95 p ercent compliance standard.    Findings were 
especially noted in four areas and appear to be res olvable through training and clear Department 
directives.   These objectives are detailed below.  
 
Findings Regarding Watch Commander Insights and Ade quacy of the Investigations 
 
Objective No. 4(c) – Watch Commander Insight  
There was a significant increase in compliance from  the prior year’s audit (from 59% to 89%).   
Findings were still identified in the Watch Command er Insight sections of the Internal Process 
Reports (IPR) regarding assessments of officer acti ons, including insufficient insight regarding 
the incident.  These Watch Commander Insights eithe r lacked the specificity necessary to 
determine which officer actions were assessed, or d id not address the applications of force used 
by the officers were not addressed.  This issue is addressed in a proposed special order currently 
in the approval cycle.      
 
Objective No. 9(e) – Adequacy of the Investigation  
Current policy requires investigations to include p hotographs of all visible officer injuries, or 
lack thereof, if relevant to the use of force, or w hen a criminal filing for a crime against a peace 
officer will be sought.  Twenty-five of the 81 inve stigations did not meet this standard.  The 
UOFRD has determined this policy to be ambiguous; t his issue is addressed in a proposed 
special order currently in the approval cycle.      
 
Findings Regarding Management Oversight 
 
There were two objectives for which management over sight did not meet the 95 percent 
standard: Objective No. 6(a) – Review of Investigat ion at Area/Division and Bureau Levels; and 
Objective No. 6(b) – Timeliness of Review of the In vestigation.  For both objectives, causes of 
findings were not systemic, but rather isolated to one or two Area/divisions or a bureau.  The 
Commanding Officer, IAID, contacted the commanding officers responsible for the majority of 
these findings.        
 
The table on the following page indicates a summary  of findings.
